<margin>A</margin> This gold is a metallic substance which is **fire** <term>ignis; in alchemy, this refers not to an open flame, but to the active, transformative heat-principle hidden within the matter</term> [16]. <unclear>Consequently</unclear>, it is gold which exists in **power** <term>potentia; the latent or "seed" state of a metal before it is matured into its final form</term> [17] and is that which is truly gold [17].

This gold is distinguished from **common gold** <term>aurum vulgi; the ordinary, "dead" metal used for currency, which lacks the "living" spirit required for alchemy</term>. It is said here that <gloss>perfected things</gloss> <note>original: "p(er)ficua"</note> are never accepted to **coagulate** <term>coagulare; the process of turning a liquid or "spirit" into a solid, stable body</term> without it. No **tincture** <term>tinctura; the potent substance—often the Philosopher's Stone—capable of transmuting metals</term> can be made without it, even if <unclear>it is mastered</unclear> and the work is completed by an easy method without common gold. Next, he expressly says here that the **Gold of the Sages** <term>aurum sophorum; the vitalized, alchemical gold used as a ferment</term> lies hidden in the nature of **putrefaction** <term>putrefactio; the essential alchemical stage of "decay" where the matter turns black and its old form is destroyed to release its essence</term>. That could also be common gold when it is naturally **digested** <term>digestio; the process of ripening or maturing a substance through gentle, consistent heat</term>; indeed, it is seen as a most highly **digested** body [45].

<meta>The previous page was noted as physically degraded and illegible, preventing a direct textual transition. However, this page clearly continues a technical discussion regarding the preparation of "Philosophical Gold," using an agricultural metaphor to explain the transition of matter from a static state to a generative one.</meta>

### ->TO THE CLOSED PALACE OF THE KING.<-

Just as when sown in a field, the name is changed to the **seed** of the farmer, which—so long as it remained in the granary—was **grain**; suited as much for breadmaking and other uses as for sowing. In like manner, **gold**, as long as it is seen in the form of a ring or a vessel, or as a coin, is "common" <note>original: *vulgare* - gold in its everyday, metallic state</note>. In the former state it is called "dead," because it would remain unchanged until the very end of the world; in the latter state it is called "living," because it is thus in **potential**. This potential is able to be brought into **action** within a few days; then, the gold will no longer be gold, but the **Chaos of the Sages** <note>original: *sophorum chaos* - a term for the "Prime Matter" or the dissolved state of the metal before its transformation</note>. Rightly, therefore, do the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>The "Philosophers" or "Sages" refer to the long lineage of alchemical authorities</note> say: **Philosophical Gold** differs from **common gold**, which difference consists in the composition. 

For just as a man is called "dead" who has already received the sentence of death: so gold is called **"living"** when it is mixed in such a composition, and placed under such a fire, in which it must necessarily receive **germinative life** <note>The power to grow or multiply, like a plant</note> shortly—indeed, within a few days it shall demonstrate the actions of beginning life. Wherefore the same <term>Sages</term>, who say their gold is **living**, command you, the seeker of the art, to **bring the dead back to life**. If you have known this, and have prepared the agent, and have rightly mixed your gold, it will not be slow to become **living**; in which "vivification" your living **menstruum** <note>original: *menstruum* - a technical term for a solvent or medium that dissolves a solid; here referring to the alchemical mercury or agent</note> will die. 

Therefore the <term>Magi</term> command [us] to **make the living dead**, and the **dead living**, and yet they call their water "living" at the very first threshold, and they say that the death of one principle with the life of the other [produces] one and the same...

# INTROITUS APERTUS
### ->AN OPEN ENTRANCE<-

...and so it reaches its completion. From this it is clear that their gold is taken in a "dead" state, while the water is "living." But by mixing these together, through a brief <term>decoction</term> <note>original: *decoctio* — a process of heating or boiling to extract essence or "ripen" a substance</note>, the dead gold is brought to life, and the living <term>Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, "Mercury" often refers to a philosophical solvent or the principle of liquidity, rather than just the common metal</note> is "killed"—that is, it is solidified or **coagulated**, the spirit being set free from the dissolved body. In this way, both rot together in the form of a sludge or slime, until every part of the mixture is torn apart into its smallest atoms.

<margin>.</margin> This, then, is the natural way of our <term>Great Work</term> <note>original: *magisterium* — the master-work of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>. The mystery that we hide so carefully is how to prepare the **Mercury** (truly so called here), which cannot be found ready-made upon the earth, and this is for <margin>4</margin> specific reasons known only to the **Adepts** <note>Those who have successfully mastered the alchemical secrets</note>. <margin>f</margin> Within this Mercury, we take pure gold—purified to the highest degree and either filed down or beaten into thin plates—and mix it into an **amalgam** as best we can. We seal this in glass and cook it continually. By the power of our "water," the gold is dissolved and returns to its "first matter," in which the life of the gold, which was previously locked away, is set free. It then takes on the life of the dissolving Mercury. In relation to the gold, this Mercury is the same as **good soil** is to a **grain of wheat**.

In this Mercury, therefore, the dissolved gold rots—and it must be so, by the necessity of nature. For this reason, after the decay of death, a new body rises again. It possesses the same essence as before, but it is of a more noble substance, which receives degrees of power according to the balance between the qualities of the **four elements** <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water; the fundamental building blocks of matter in pre-modern science</note>. This is the logic of our work; this is our entire philosophy. We have said, therefore, that nothing happens in our work se-

<meta>The catchword "cretum" indicates the next word is "secretum" (secretly or apart from), likely concluding the sentence "nothing happens in our work secretly" or "without nature."</meta>

It emphasizes that the gold must be reduced to its "first matter" through putrefaction to attain a more noble, powerful state.</summary>

...as far as they are a creature of **GOD**, far be it [from us to worship them]; indeed, to that extent we honor them and deem them worthy of honor; but the people, both the Israelites and the worldly, adore them. My heart murmurs unheard things; my spirit beats in my chest for the good of the whole Israel of **GOD**. I send these things forth into the world like a herald, so that I may not be buried in the world as one who was useless. ## CHAPTER XIV.

->*Concerning the requirements in general regarding the accidental circumstances for this work.*<-

We have separated the chemical art from all common errors, and having defeated the sophistries and the curious dreams of would-be experts, we have taught that the art must be made from <term>gold<note>original: "auro"</note></term> and <term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurio"</note></term>. We have added reasons so clear and transparent that, unless you wished to be blind even in the presence of the Sun, you could not fail to perceive them. We have professed, and we profess again, that we have not brought these things forward based on the faith we give to the writings of others; we have seen and we have known what we faithfully declare; we have made, seen, and we hold the <term>Great Stone Elixir<note>original: "Lapidem magnum Elixirem," referring to the Philosopher's Stone in its most potent form.</note></term>. Nor indeed do we begrudge you the knowledge of it, but rather we hope that you may learn it thoroughly from these writings. We have notified you, moreover, that the preparation of the true <term>Philosophical Mercury</term> is difficult—so difficult that there is need for a special grace of GOD, if anyone should desire to arrive at an exact knowledge of it. ## CHAPTER XV.

### *Concerning the accidental purging of Mercury and gold.*

<margin>#</margin>Perfect gold is found in the bowels of the earth, from which it is sometimes recovered in small fragments and sand. If you can obtain this in its natural state, it is pure enough; if not, purify it either with <term>Antimony<note>A semi-metal used in alchemy to "devour" impurities in gold, leaving the gold refined.</note></term>, or by <term>cupellation<note>original: "per cineritium"; a refining process where metals are heated in a "cupel" made of bone ash, which absorbs base metal oxides.</note></term>, or by boiling it with <term>aqua fortis<note>Nitric acid, used to dissolve silver and other base metals away from gold.</note></term>, provided the gold is first <term>granulated<note>The process of pouring molten metal into water to create small, high-surface-area beads.</note></term>. Afterward, melt it in a crucible, file it, and it is ready.

But <term>Mercury<note>original: "Mercurius"</note></term> requires an internal and essential purification, which is the addition of true <term>sulfur<note>In this context, "sulfur" refers to the active, combustible principle or "seed" added to the mercury.</note></term> bit by bit, according to the number of the <term>eagles<note>original: "aquilarum." Philalethes uses "eagles" as a code for the number of times the mercury is distilled or purified with the "metallic sword" (often iron or antimony).</note></term>; then it is purged to its very roots. But beyond this, it requires an accidental cleansing to wash away the external filth ejected from its center. This labor is not absolutely necessary, yet it speeds up the work, and therefore it is beneficial. For this reason, take your Mercury,

->### TO THE CLOSED PALACE OF THE KING.<-

...which you have prepared through a suitable number of eagles <note>In alchemy, an "eagle" refers to a specific cycle of sublimation and distillation used to purify mercury</note>, and sublime it three times from common salt and the scoriae <note>slag or dross</note> of Mars <note>In the language of alchemy, Mars represents the metal Iron</note>. Grind these together with vinegar and a small amount of sal ammoniac <note>Ammonium chloride</note> until the Mercury disappears from sight. Once it is dried, distill it through a glass **retort** <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note> using a fire that is gradually increased in heat until all the Mercury has ascended. Repeat this process four times. 

Afterward, boil the Mercury in spirit of vinegar for one hour in a **cucurbit** <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel</note> or a glass vessel with a wide bottom and a narrow neck, shaking it vigorously from time to time. Then, decant the vinegar and wash away the acidity with spring water, pouring it over the substance repeatedly. Finally, dry the Mercury, and you will marvel at its brilliance. 

You could also choose to wash it with urine or a mixture of vinegar and salt, thereby skipping the sublimation. In that case, you must distill it at least four times after you have completed all the "eagles" without any further additions. Each time, you must wash the steel retort with ash and water. At the end, boil it in distilled vinegar for half a day, shaking it vigorously at intervals; pour off the blackened vinegar and add fresh vinegar. Finally, wash it with hot water. You may also free the spirit of vinegar from its blackness by re-distilling it, allowing it to retain its original power. 

All of this effort is intended to remove the external impurity which, while it does not cling to the center of the metal, is nevertheless quite stubborn upon the surface. You will perceive this impurity in the following way: if you amalgamate <note>To blend mercury with another metal</note> this Mercury with the most purified gold upon a very clean piece of paper, you will see that the **amalgam** stains the paper with a dark blackness. You counteract this dross through the aforementioned distillation.

## CHAPTER XVI

### *On the amalgam of Mercury and gold, and on the required weight of each.*

Once these things have been properly performed, take one part of purified gold, either beaten into thin leaves or finely filed, and two parts of Mercury. Place these into a marble mortar <note>a sturdy bowl used for grinding</note> that has been heated—specifically by being placed in boiling water (from which it is dried immediately upon removal, yet retains its heat for a long time). Grind the mixture with a pestle made of ivory, glass, stone, iron (though iron is not as good), or boxwood; however, glass or stone is superior. I personally am accustomed to using a white coral pestle. 

Grind, I say, vigorously, until the mixture becomes impalpable <note>so finely ground that no grit can be felt between the fingers</note>. Grind with the same diligence that painters use when crushing their pigments. Then, observe the consistency <note>original: *temperaturam*, here referring to the physical state or "temper" of the mixture</note>; if it is as pliable as butter—neither too warm nor too cold—and if, when the amalgam <note>a blend of mercury and another metal</note> is tilted, it does not allow the Mercury to run off like the fluid of a dropsy <note>original: *aqua hydropicæ intercutalis*; an old medical term for the fluid buildup in edema</note> beneath the skin, then the consistency is good. 

If it is not so, add enough Mercury to achieve this state. The rule of the mixture is this: it must be most easily pliable and very soft, yet still capable of being formed into round globules, much like butter, which...

Although it yields to the lightest touch of a finger, it can nevertheless be formed into balls by a "washing woman" <note>This refers to the alchemical trope of "woman’s work" (opus mulierum), suggesting a stage of the process that requires patience and cleansing rather than intense heat.</note>. Observe the example cited as the most exact: for just like butter, even if it is tilted, it does not pour out any liquid part separate from the whole mass; our mixture is exactly the same. For the intrinsic nature of <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: Mercurius; quicksilver</note>, this sign will be given either in a double or triple proportion of Mercury to the <term>body</term> <note>The "body" refers to the solid metal being dissolved, in this case, Gold.</note>, or even in a triple part of the body to a quadruple part of the spirit, or a double to a triple part. The <term>amalgam</term> <note>A mixture of mercury with another metal, often creating a pasty consistency.</note> will be softer or harsher according to the variation of the Mercury; always remember, however, that it should coalesce into small clumps. When these clumps are set aside, they should harden in such a way that no "living" Mercury <note>original: vivacior Mercurius; liquid, uncombined quicksilver.</note> appears at the bottom more than at the top. Note, indeed, that if the amalgam is permitted to rest, it hardens of its own accord. Therefore, the consistency must be judged while it is being stirred; if it is then as pliable as butter, and allows small clumps to be made, and if its clumps—placed on clean paper—harden in stillness without trouble so that the bottom is no more liquid than the top, the proportion is good. Once this is done, take <term>spirit of vinegar</term> <note>Concentrated acetic acid.</note> and dissolve in it a third part of <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>Ammonium chloride, used as a flux and cleaning agent.</note>, and place the Mercury and <term>the Sun</term> <note>original: Sol; the alchemical name for Gold.</note> previously amalgamated into this liquor. Place it in a long-necked glass vessel and allow it to boil for a quarter of an hour with a strong boiling. Then remove the mixture from the glass, set aside the liquid, heat the mortar, and grind it, as described above,

...less, if you can; but prepare it near that measurement as carefully as you can. Let the glass have a neck the height of one palm <note>A 'palm' was a traditional unit of measurement, roughly 3 to 4 inches.</note>; let the glass be very clear and thick—the thicker, the better—provided that you can distinguish the actions within the hollow of the glass. <margin>f.</margin> It should not be thicker in one place than in another. Let the material adapted for this glass be half an ounce <term>uncia: a unit of weight, approximately 28 grams, the ancestor of the modern ounce</term> of gold with one ounce of Mercury, and if you add triple the Mercury, the whole composition will still be within two ounces. And this is an exquisite proportion. Furthermore, unless the glass is thick, it will not be able to endure in the fire, because the winds <note>This refers to the internal gas pressure and vapors generated by heating the chemicals.</note> that are formed in the vessel from our embryo <note>In alchemy, the 'embryo' refers to the developing chemical mixture or the 'Philosopher's Stone' in its early, volatile state.</note> will burst the vessel.

Let the glass be sealed at the top with care, so that there is no crack, let alone a hole; otherwise, the work would perish. Thus you will see that the work, in its material beginnings, does not exceed the price of three gold pieces <note>original: "aureorum"</note>. Indeed, in the production of the water, the cost of one pound barely exceeds two crowns <note>original: "coronatos", a gold or silver coin used in early modern Europe</note>. The instruments, I admit, are several; however, they are by no means expensive, and if you have my distillation instrument <term>destillatorium: a specialized apparatus for refining substances through heating and condensation</term>, you will easily be spared from fragile glasses. There are some, however, who dream that perhaps the price of one imperial coin <note>original: "imperialis"</note> would serve the whole work; to whom it may be answered that this proves they have never completed the work by experiment. For there are other things necessary in the work which require expenditures. But these men will insist, following the Philosophers <note>Here, 'Philosophers' refers to the established alchemical authorities and authors of the Hermetic tradition.</note>, that "everything which is bought at a great price is found to be a lie in our work." ### THE OPEN ENTRANCE

What is our work? Truly, is it to make the stone?
<margin>#</margin> That indeed is the final goal: but the true work is to find the moisture <note>original: "humiditatem"; in alchemy, this refers to the 'Universal Solvent' or 'Secret Fire' that can dissolve gold without violence.</note> in which gold melts just as ice does in warm water. To find this is our work. In this search, many exhaust themselves toiling so that they might gain the Mercury of the Sun <note>Gold that has been processed into a liquid alchemical state.</note>, and others the Mercury of the Moon <note>Silver processed into an alchemical state.</note>; but they do so in vain. For in this work, everything that is sold for a high price is a lie. I say truly, that as much of the material principle of this water can be bought for the price of a single florin <note>A gold coin used throughout Europe; the author is emphasizing that the "secret ingredient" is common and inexpensive.</note> as would be enough to animate two whole pounds of Mercury, so that it becomes the true Mercury of the Sages. Meanwhile, the glass vessels, the coals, the earthenware containers, the furnace, and the iron tools and instruments cannot be obtained for nothing. Therefore, let the base chatter of the Sophists <note>The author uses 'Sophists' to describe "false" alchemists or charlatans who use complex language to deceive people or who work with the wrong materials.</note> be silent—those who lie shamelessly and lead so many astray with their talk. <margin>〰</margin> Without a perfect body, our copper <margin>/</margin>—namely gold—no tincture <note>original: "tinctura"; the substance or "dye" capable of transforming base metals into gold.</note> <insert>can</insert> ever be obtained; and our stone is, on one side, cheap, immature, and volatile, and on the other, perfect, precious, and fixed <note>'Fixed' refers to a substance that can endure the heat of fire without evaporating.</note>. These two species are the body (gold) and the spirit, namely quicksilver <note>original: "argentum vivum", literally "living silver" or mercury.</note>.

<meta>catchword: CHAPTER</meta>

<summary>The author defines the 'Great Work' as finding a specific solvent that dissolves gold like warm water melts ice, emphasizing that the primary ingredient is inexpensive despite the cost of lab equipment. He warns against charlatans and explains that the Philosopher's Stone is a paradox composed of both 'perfect' gold and 'volatile' mercury.</summary>
<keywords>Great Work, Philosopher's Stone, Alchemical Mercury, Mercury of the Sages, Gold, Quicksilver, Sophists, Universal Solvent, Tincture</keywords>

## CHAPTER XVIII.

### *On the Furnace or the Philosophical Athanor.*

We have spoken of Mercury, and of its preparation, proportion, and virtue; likewise of Sulphur, and of its necessity and use in our work. I have advised how these things are to be prepared and taught how they are to be mixed; I have also revealed much concerning the vessel in which they are to be sealed. I warn you that all these things must be understood with a grain of salt <note>original: "cum grano salis"; a warning that the text uses allegorical language rather than literal laboratory recipes</note>, lest by proceeding too literally you happen to wander frequently into error. For we have woven these philosophical subtleties with an unusual clarity, yet unless you have "scented out" the many metaphors in the preceding chapters, you will harvest scarcely anything for your harvest besides your own loss. 

As an example, therefore, where we have said without any ambiguity that one of the principles is Mercury and the other is the <term>Sun<gloss>Sol, or Gold; in alchemy, the Sun represents the perfected masculine principle and the metal Gold.</gloss></term>—one being available for purchase, the other to be fashioned by art: Know that our Mercury gives gold from itself. If you do not recognize this as the subject of our secrets, you must sell it for common Gold. In every test, it is true Gold, and for that reason it is "salable"—that is, it can be sold to anyone without a shred of doubt. Our Sun is therefore commonly available for sale, but it is not commonly bought, because <margin>☩</margin> for it to become "ours," there is a need for our art. You may find our Sun in common Gold and in the <term>Moon<gloss>Luna, or Silver; the feminine principle in the alchemical wedding.</gloss></term>

<meta>catchword: repe- (likely "reperire," meaning "to find")</meta>

<summary>In this chapter, the author warns the reader to look beyond literal interpretations and metaphors while discussing the philosophical nature of Mercury and Gold (the Sun). He emphasizes that while the materials may be common, they only become the "philosophers' materials" through the specific application of alchemical art.</summary>

<keywords>Athanor, Mercury, Sulphur, Sol, Luna, philosophical gold, alchemical metaphor, hermetic vessel</keywords>

...to find; I myself have sought and found in these things. But it is by no means an easy task. The Stone itself is to be fashioned with easier effort than that with which you will find the <term>proximate matter<gloss>Materia proxima; the immediate starting material that requires the least processing to become the Philosopher's Stone.</gloss></term> of the Stone in buying common gold.
<margin>#</margin> Therefore, our Gold is the proximate matter of our Stone; common gold is near, other metals are remote, and those things which are not metallic are most remote, or rather, foreign. Because our Gold is <term>Chaos<gloss>A term for the 'Prima Materia'—the primordial, unformed substance that contains the seeds of all things.</gloss></term>, whose soul has not been chased away by fire. Common gold is that whose soul has retreated into a closed citadel so that it might be safe from the fiery tyranny of <term>Vulcan<gloss>The Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this represents the harsh, external heat of a furnace that "locks" the essence of common metals.</gloss></term>.
<margin>d</margin> But if you seek our Gold in a "middle thing"—between the perfect and the imperfect—seek and you will find: otherwise, you must loosen the bars of common gold. This is called the "first preparation," by which the enchantment <note>original: "incantamentum"; referring to the physical bonds that hold the metallic spirit captive</note> of its body is dissolved, without which it cannot complete the work of the <term>husband<gloss>The masculine principle in the alchemical marriage, usually representing Sulphur or Gold, which must join with the feminine Mercury.</gloss></term>. If you have entered upon the former way, you are bound to proceed with a most gentle fire; but if the latter, then you must implore the help of scorching Vulcan. You must employ such a fire, for instance, as we supply in the stage of multiplication, while the physical Sun or Moon of the common sort is used as a <term>ferment<gloss>A substance added to the alchemical mixture to "leaven" it, directing the transformation toward either gold or silver.</gloss></term> for perfecting the Elixir. This will surely be a labyrinth unless you know how to extricate yourself.
<margin>q</margin> Yet in any progress you require an equal and continuous heat, whether you have worked with the common Sun or with ours. Know both: that our Sun will give you the work perfected two or three months sooner than common gold, and the Elixir will be in...

...in its first perfection, its power is thousand-fold, whereas in another work it will barely be hundred-fold. Furthermore, if you complete the work with our Sun <term>Sol: In alchemy, "our Sun" usually refers to "Philosophical Gold" or the principle of Sulfur, rather than the common metal</term>, you must feed it, saturate it, ferment it, and so on, by which its power will grow immensely; in another work, however, you must illuminate and wax it <term>incerare: the process of softening a substance until it has the consistency of wax</term>, as is taught abundantly in the *Great Rosary* <note>original: "Rosario magno"; likely referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum*, a famous 16th-century alchemical treatise</note>.

Moreover, if you have worked with our Sun, you can calcine, putrefy, and whiten it by a gentle internal fire, aided <gloss>together</gloss> with a dewy warmth applied from the outside. If you have worked with the common Sun <note>ordinary gold</note>, the materials must be prepared by subliming and boiling, so that later you may unite them with the virgin's milk <term>Virgin's Milk: A metaphorical term for a white liquid solvent, often a mercurial water or a specific acidic distillate</term>. However you make progress, you will be able to accomplish nothing without fire.

Therefore, not without reason did the truthful Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the hermetic and alchemical tradition</note> establish fire as the governor of the work, second only to the Sun and Moon <note>original: "Soli Lunæque"; referring to the male and female principles of Gold and Silver</term>. I would wish this fire, however, to be understood as coming from our truly secret furnace, which the common eye has never seen. There is, however, also another furnace, which we call the common one, which is constructed either of brick, or of potter's clay, or of iron and bronze plates well-coated with clay. This furnace we call the Athanor <term>Athanor: A self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a consistent temperature for long periods</term>, whose form most pleasing to me is that of a tower with a nest. Therefore, let there be a tower about three feet in height and nine inches (or a common span) wide; after the base or foundational layer, let there be a small door for clearing out the ashes, three or four inches [wide] on all sides, with a stone to [be fitted] <meta>catchword: aptato</meta>

...having been fitted, above which a grate should immediately be placed; a little above the grate let there be two openings, about two fingers wide, through which the heat may be released into the attached Athanor <term>Athanor: A self-feeding furnace designed by alchemists to maintain a steady, continuous temperature over long periods without constant supervision</term>. Moreover, let the tower be sealed exactly against any cracks; at the top, coals are to be introduced—the first ones lit, then others thrown in—and finally, let the opening be sealed tightly. With such a furnace, you may complete the work according to your heart's desire. Furthermore, if you are inquisitive, you may find various other ways to manage the required fire. 

Let the Athanor therefore be made in this way, so that after the material has been placed inside, you can apply any degree of heat without removing the glass vessel, ranging as you wish from a feverish warmth <note>original: "calore febrili"; a low, steady heat comparable to the temperature of a human body in fever</note> up to the fire of a lesser reverberatory furnace <term>Reverberatory fire: A high-heat setting where the flames or heat are reflected—or "reverberated"—off the furnace roof onto the vessel</term>, and in its most intense stage, it should last by itself for at least ten or twelve hours. Then the door of the work lies open to you. But when you have already obtained the Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>, you might more usefully fashion a portable furnace, because once the Stone has been made, it is multiplied in less time and with a more gentle, natural fire.

<margin>#</margin>...although at the beginning of this book I had decided to bury [this] in deep silence. This is the one great sophism <term>sophisma; a clever but deceptive argument used by alchemists to hide the truth from the unworthy</term> of all the adepts: some speak of the gold and silver of the common people <term>auro argentoque vulgi; ordinary metallic gold and silver</term>, and they speak truly; and others deny the same, and they also speak truly. Moved by charity, I now extend my hand, and I call upon all the adepts, and I accuse them all of envy. I too had decided to tread the same path of envy, except that GOD turned us aside against our own plan—to whom be eternal sanctification!

I say therefore that both ways are true, because the way is only one in the end, but not in the beginning. For the whole work is in our Mercury <term>Mercurio nostro; the secret "philosophical" solvent</term> and our Sun <term>Sole nostro; the sulfur or "seed" of gold</term>. Our Mercury is our way, and without it nothing will happen. Also, our Sun <margin>☛</margin> is not the gold of the common people, and yet it is contained within it. And if you have worked in our Mercury with the gold of the common people <margin>#</margin> using the proper regimen <term>regimine; the precise control of heat and timing in the alchemical furnace</term>, from these, in one hundred and fifty days, you will have our gold, because <margin>X</margin> our Sun is produced from our Mercury. 

<margin>o</margin> Therefore, if the gold of the common people has been broken down into its elements by our Mercury, and joined together again, the entire mixture, by the benefit of the fire, will become our gold. If that gold is then boiled again through Mercury, it will certainly give all the signs described by the Philosophers, using such a fire as they themselves described. 

Now truly, if you apply the regimen of the Stone <term>regimen lapidis; the specific heating process used to create the Philosopher's Stone</term> to the boiling of common gold (however pure it may be) with our Mercury, you are certainly in the way of error. And in this consists that great Labyrinth in which novices <note>original: "tyrones"; refers to beginners or recruits in the alchemical art</note>...

<margin>#</margin> almost all practitioners are stuck, because the Philosophers in their books write of two paths, which in truth are but one way, except that one is more direct than the other. <margin>4</margin> Those who write about the **common Sun** <note>original: "Sole vulgi"; ordinary physical gold</note>, as we do in this little treatise—as well as Artephius, Flamel, Ripley, and many others—we are to be understood in no other way than that the **Philosophical Sun** <note>The perfected gold or "Sulfur" of the Wise</note> is made from common gold and **our Mercury** <term>Mercurius noster; the secret solvent or "living water" used by alchemists to dissolve metals</term>, which then through <gloss>x x x x x x</gloss> repeated liquefaction will give <gloss>x x x x x x x x x x</gloss> **Sulfur** and fixed, incombustible **Quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum vivum fixum"</note>, which colors and transforms all things in the testing.

<margin>*</margin> Likewise, by this way of understanding, **our Stone** is in every metal and mineral, because, for instance, the common Sun can be extracted from them, from which our "proximate" Sun may be sought. In all common metals, you see, is our Sun; but in gold and silver it is contained more closely. Therefore, says **Flamel** <note>Nicolas Flamel, a famous 14th-century French scrivener and legendary alchemist</note>, some have labored in **Jupiter** <note>The alchemical name for Tin</note>, and others in **Saturn** <note>The alchemical name for Lead</note>; but I, he says, labored in the **Sun** <note>Gold</note> and found it. Yet there is one thing in the metallic kingdom, <margin>2</margin> of wondrous origin, in which our Sun is more proximate than in the common Sun and Moon <note>Gold and Silver</note>, if you seek it in the <margin>A r 2</margin> hour of its birth—which liquefies in **our Mercury** <margin>3</margin> like ice in warm water; and yet it is in some way likened to gold. <margin>C</margin> You will not find this immediately in common gold, <margin>2</margin> but from it, through our Mercury, to be digested for one hundred and fifty days, you will find this true and same matter, which is our gold, sought by the longer way, and not yet—

...hither so powerful, and that which nature has left at hand. And yet by turning the wheel a third time, you will find the same thing in both, though with this difference: in the former [the shorter path], it is accomplished in seven months; in the latter, in the space of a year and a half. I am experienced in both ways, yet I praise the easier path; nonetheless, I have described the more difficult one, lest I should draw the curse of all the **Wise** <note>original: "Sophorum"; the community of alchemical masters</note> upon my head.

<margin>q.</margin> Know therefore, that this is the only difficulty in reading the books of the more honest men: that they all, to a man, vary the **regimen** <term>regimen; the method of governing the heat and timing of the various stages of the alchemical process</term>. And even when they speak of one specific work, they teach the regimen of another. Ensnared by this net, I remained stuck for a long time before I could free my feet from the snare.

I notify you, therefore, that the heat in our work is most gentle by nature, provided you understand our work correctly. But if you work with the **Sun of the commoners** <note>original: "Sole vulgi"; ordinary physical gold</note>, that work is not strictly our work; yet it will lead straight to our work at its determined time.

<margin>A</margin> In that path [using common gold], you truly need a strong **digestion** <note>the process of heating a substance to achieve maturity or transformation</note> and a proportionate fire. But afterward, you shall proceed with a most gentle fire in our **turreted athanor** <term>athanore turrali; a self-feeding, tower-like furnace designed to maintain constant, steady heat over weeks or months</term>, which I cannot praise highly enough.

### CHAPTER XX.

### *On the appearance of blackness in the work of the Sun and Moon.*

If you have worked with the Sun <note>Gold</note> and the Moon <note>Silver</note> so that you might seek our sulphur in them, observe whether you see your matter swollen like dough, bubbling like water, or rather like liquid pitch. For our Sun and our Mercury <term>Mercury: the fluid agent or "solvent" used to break down metals</term> have their symbolic form in the work of common gold combined with our Mercury. Once the furnace is lit, wait in the bubbling heat for twenty days, during which time you will observe various colors; but around the end of the fourth week, provided the heat has been continuous, you will see a lovely greenness <note>In alchemy, the "Green Lion" stage often signifies the beginning of the matter's life and its power to dissolve</note>, which will not disappear for about ten days. Rejoice then, for certainly you will soon see everything become as black as coal, and all the members of your compound will be reduced to atoms. For this operation is nothing other than the resolution of the fixed <note>The solid, stable part of the metal</note> into the non-fixed <note>The volatile or liquid part</note>, so that both, being joined afterwards, may form one matter that is partly spiritual and partly corporeal. Wherefore the Philosopher says:

Take the Corascene dog <note>original: "canem Coraſcenum"; an alchemical cipher, likely referring to a specific mineral or "crude" metallic state</note> and the Armenian bitch <note>original: "Caniculam Armeniae"; representing a second metallic substance, often associated with the receptive or "feminine" principle in the reaction</note>, join them together, and they will give birth for you to a son the color of the sky. For these natures, by a brief boiling, will be turned into a broth like the foam of the sea, or a thick cloud, which will be tinged with a leaden color. And I swear to you in good faith that I have hidden nothing except the regimen <term>Regimen: the precise management of the furnace heat and the timing of each stage</term>; however, if you are wise, you will very easily gather this from my words.

Once the regimen is known, take up the stone shown above and govern it as you know how, and these notable things will follow. First, as soon as the stone feels its fire, the Sulphur and Mercury will flow together over the fire like wax, and the Sulphur will be burned, and it will change colors from day to day; meanwhile, the Mercury will be incombustible, except that it will be tinged by the colors of the Sulphur for a time. Yet it will not be tainted, and therefore it will thoroughly wash the Lato <term>Lato: an alchemical term for the base or "dirty" metal that must be purified into gold</term> from all its filth. 

Repeat the heaven above the earth <note>This refers to the process of sublimation or distillation, where the volatile "heavenly" spirit rises and then falls back onto the solid "earthly" matter</note> so many times until the earth has conceived a heavenly nature. O Holy Nature! who alone does that which is utterly impossible for any man. For that reason, when you see in your glass the natures mixed together like clotted and burnt blood, be certain that the woman has endured the embrace of the male <note>An alchemical metaphor for the union of the volatile (female) and fixed (male) elements</note>. Therefore, from the first drying of your matter, expect within seventeen days that the two natures will be converted into a fattened broth, which together will swirl like a thick cloud or sea foam, as has been said, the color of which will be very dark.

->Toward the Closing of the King's Palace.<-

Then hold firmly onto the <term>royal offspring<note>original: "prolem regiam"; a symbolic term for the developing alchemical substance</note></term> that has been conceived, because from it you will see green, yellow, black, and blue vapors in the fire and on the sides of the vessel. These are the "winds" which occur frequently during the formation of our <term>embryo<note>original: "embryone nostro"; the early state of the Philosophers' Stone</note></term>, and they must be cautiously retained, lest they escape and the work be destroyed. Beware also of the odor; do not let it exhale through any crack, for the power of the <term>stone<note>original: "lapidis"; the Philosophers' Stone</note></term> would suffer significant harm from it.

For this reason, the <term>Philosopher<note>original: "Philosophus"; likely referring to an authority like Hermes Trismegistus or an anonymous master of the art</note></term> orders the vessel to be kept cautiously with its seal, and you are warned: do not cease from the work, nor move the vessel, nor open it, nor interrupt the <term>decoction<note>original: "decoctionem"; the process of heating or boiling the matter</note></term> at any time. Instead, continue boiling until you see the moisture fail, which will happen within thirty days; then rejoice, and be certain that you have walked the right path. Watch the work closely then, because perhaps within two weeks from that time you will see the whole earth dry and remarkably black. 

Then the death of the compound is at hand; the "winds" have ceased, and all things have given themselves to rest. This is that great <term>eclipse of the Sun and Moon together<note>original: "eclipsis Solis & Lunæ simul"; a symbol for the union and "death" of the masculine (sulfur) and feminine (mercury) principles</note></term>, in which no light shall shine upon the earth, and the sea shall disappear. Our <term>Chaos<note>original: "Chaos"; the primal, unformed state of matter before it is purified</note></term> is then completed, from which—by the command of **GOD**—all the wonders of the world shall emerge in their proper order.

## CHAPTER XXI.

### *Concerning the burning of the flowers and the caution required.*

The <term>burning of the flowers<note>original: "florum combustio"; a metaphorical term for overheating the chemical matter during a delicate stage of development, which prevents the proper progression of colors</note></term> is no small error, and yet it is one easily committed before the tender natures are properly extracted from their depths. This error must be especially guarded against after the third week. For in the beginning, there is such an <term>abundance of moisture<note>original: "humoris copia"</note></term> that if you direct the work with a fire stronger than is proper, the fragile vessel will not withstand the force of the <term>"winds"<note>Vapors or gases produced by heating the substances within the sealed glass</note></term> and will immediately burst—unless, perhaps, your vessel is exceptionally large. And in that case, the moisture would be scattered to such an extent that it would no longer return to its "body," or at least not enough would remain to revive and nourish it. 

However, when the "earth" begins to retain a portion of its water, and the vapors consequently begin to fail, the fire can indeed be intensified beyond measure without any risk of breaking the vessel; but the work will be ruined nonetheless. It will take on the color of a <term>wild poppy<note>original: "papaveris sylvestris"; likely referring to a premature or "false" redness that signifies the matter has been scorched rather than matured</note></term>, and the entire compound will eventually become a <term>dry powder<note>original: "pulvis siccus"</note></term>, reddened to no purpose. You shall judge by this sign that the fire was stronger than was just—so much so, in fact, that it was an enemy to the true conjunction. For you must know that our work requires a true <term>transformation of natures<note>original: "naturarum mutationem"</note></term>, which cannot take place unless a final union of both natures occurs; yet they cannot be united except in the form of water. For there is no union of solid bodies; but at least

<summary>This chapter warns the alchemist against using excessive heat after the third week of the process, which can lead to the 'burning of the flowers'—a state where the matter turns into a useless red powder instead of achieving a true liquid union.</summary>

### TO THE CLOSED PALACE OF THE KING

Crushing or grinding cannot achieve a perfect microscopic union <note>original: *unio per minima*; an alchemical concept of total integration at the atomic level</note> of the body with the spirit; yet spirits can indeed be easily united among themselves. For this reason, a homogeneous metallic water is required, for which a path is prepared through a preliminary <term>calcination</term> <note>the process of heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a fine powder</note>. Therefore, this "drying" is not truly a drying, but rather a reduction of the water and earth—through the sieve of nature—into atoms finer than what water alone requires, so that the earth may receive the transmutative ferment of the water.

However, if subjected to a heat more vehement than is proper, this spiritual nature is struck as if by the hammer of death; it turns from active to passive, and from spiritual to corporal—namely, a useless **red precipitate**. This is because, at its proper heat, the color becomes a **raven-like blackness** <note>the *nigredo* stage, symbolizing the "death" of the old matter before rebirth</note>, which, although dark, is a most highly desired color.

Nevertheless, a redness <note>original: *rubedo*</note> is visible at the very beginning of the true work, and it is quite remarkable; when this appears alongside the proper amount of moisture, it shows that **heaven has lain with the earth** and has conceived the fire of nature. For this reason, the entire hollow of the glass vessel will be tinted with a golden color. But this color will not last; it will soon give birth to green, and then you should expect black within a short time. If you remain patient, you will see your wish fulfilled.

At any rate, **make haste slowly** <note>original: *festina lentè*; a favorite maxim of alchemists suggesting that while the process shouldn't stall, rushing it with too much heat causes failure</note>, and yet maintain a sufficiently strong fire. Like a skilled pilot, steer your ship between Scylla and Charybdis <note>the mythological monsters representing two equally dangerous extremes</note> <margin>#</margin> if you desire to gain the riches of both the East and West Indies. At times, you will see what look like tiny islands—multicolored spikes and umbrellas <note>likely referring to crystalline or floral-like patterns forming on the surface of the liquid</note>—emerging in the waves and at the sides, which will soon dissolve, and others [will appear]...

<summary>This page describes the delicate chemical and spiritual transition of matter within the alchemical vessel, emphasizing that the "raven's blackness" (nigredo) is the first sign of success. The author warns that excessive heat will kill the "spirit" of the work, resulting in a useless red powder rather than the desired transformation.</summary>

## CHAPTER XXII.

### *The Rule of Saturn: what it is and why it is so called.*

As many of the wise Sages <term>Magi</term> <note>The 'Sophic Magi' refers to the historical masters of the 'Great Work' or alchemy.</note> as have written about this labor have spoken of the work and rule of Saturn <term>Saturn</term> <note>In alchemical symbolism, Saturn corresponds to the metal lead and the color black; the 'Rule of Saturn' refers to the stage where the matter is 'blacker than black.'</note>. Some, understanding them incorrectly, have turned aside into various errors and deceived themselves by their own opinions. Because of this, some were led away to work upon common lead with the greatest of hope, but the least bit of fruit. But you should know that our lead <term>our lead</term> <note>original: *plumbum nostrum*; a common alchemical phrase emphasizing that their 'lead' is a specialized philosophical substance, not the common metal found in a mine.</note> is worthier than any gold. It is the slime in which the soul of gold is joined with Mercury, so that they may afterwards produce Adam and his wife Eve <note>Adam and Eve here represent the masculine (Sulfur) and feminine (Mercury) principles which must unite to produce the 'Stone.'</note>. Therefore, since the highest has humbled itself here so that it might become the lowest, one must expect [the soul]...

# CHAPTER XXIII.
## *On the various governments of this work.*

Be assured for certain, studious novice <note>original: *tyro*; an apprentice or beginner in the art.</note>, that nothing in the entire work of the stone <term>Stone: The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the Great Work.</term> has been hidden except for the regimen <term>Regimen: The controlled application of heat and the sequence of stages—often likened to "governing" a kingdom.</term>, concerning which that saying of the Philosopher is true: *Whoever shall know this scientifically, the princes and magnates of the earth shall honor him.* And I swear to you in good faith that if this alone were made public, even fools would laugh at the art. For once it is understood, the whole thing is nothing other than "women’s work and children's play," <note>A famous alchemical maxim suggesting that once the secret of the fire is known, the process requires only the simple patience of a woman tending a hearth or a child playing.</note> which is to say, to cook. Therefore, the Sages <note>original: *Sophi*; the enlightened masters of alchemy.</note> have hidden this secret with the highest art, and you should firmly believe that we have fundamentally done the same; yet, because I have proposed and promised clarity in this little treatise, it is necessary to do at least something, lest the minds of the readers—

<meta>catchword: niofo</meta>

The author describes the work, once understood, as being as simple as cooking or children's play.</summary>

<margin>☞</margin>you should know that our regimen in the whole work is a single linear path, that is, to decoct <term>Decoct: To boil or heat a substance to extract its essence or concentrate it.</term> and to digest; and yet this one regimen embraces many others within itself, which the envious <note>The "envious" (invidiosi) is a standard alchemical term for previous authors who intentionally hid secrets to prevent the unworthy from succeeding.</note> have hidden under a diversity of names and described as if they were various operations. We, for the sake of our promised openness, will make a manifestation far more clear, for which you will admit our unusual honesty in this matter.

## CHAPTER XXIV.

...fifty <note>The text begins mid-word: *quinquaginta* (fifty)</note>, during which time, without a leader, the wretched beginners <note>original: *tyrones*; apprentices or novices in the alchemical art</note> devote themselves to uncertain experiments. From the arrival of the blackness <note>original: *nigredo*; the first major stage of the alchemical Great Work, symbolizing the "death" or putrefaction of the matter</note> until the end of the work, new signs appearing daily refresh the craftsman enough; but I confess it is tedious to wander here for fifty days without a leader, a sign, or a guide. I say, therefore, that the entire interval of time from the first firing until the blackness is the regimen of Mercury; of the Mercury of the Wise <note>original: *Mercurii Sophici*; "Sophic" or "Philosophical" Mercury is the secret, purified substance of the alchemists, distinct from common quicksilver</note>, I say, who alone works throughout that whole time while his companion remains dead for a suitable space of time—and no one before me has revealed this. 

Therefore, when the materials are joined—which are the Gold <note>original: *Sol*; literally "the Sun," representing the fixed, masculine principle or pure gold</note> and our Mercury—do not believe with the common crowd of Alchemists that the "setting of the Sun" <note>An alchemical metaphor for the dissolution and darkening of the gold</note> happens quickly. Not at all; we have waited much and long before patience was established between the water and the fire, and the envious <note>original: *invidi*; traditional term for alchemical authors who deliberately hid secrets from the "unworthy"</note> have included this under a brief name when they called their matter in the first work *Rebis* <note>original: *Rebis*; from the Latin *res bina*, meaning "a double thing" or "two-thing," symbolizing the union of opposites like male and female, or fixed and volatile</note>, that is, a thing made from two things, according to the Poet:

> The thing Rebis is twofold joined, yet nevertheless one,
> It is dissolved so that at first there may be either Gold or the seeds of Silver <note>original: *Sol aut spermata Lunae*; literally "Sun or the seeds of the Moon," representing the primary components of the work</note>.

Therefore, know for certain that although our Mercury devours the Gold, it is not in the way the chemical pretenders <note>original: *Philosophastri*; a derogatory term for false or amateur alchemists</note> think. For even if you join the Gold with our Mercury, you will recover it from there safe and possessing its original virtue after waiting a year, unless you cook it in a suitable degree of fire. <summary>Philalethes explains that the "Regimen of Mercury" lasts fifty days and is a period of waiting where the Mercury of the Wise acts upon the "dead" Gold. He clarifies the concept of the Rebis and warns that without the correct heat, the materials will not truly transform.</summary>

-># OPEN ENTRANCE<-

...asserts the opposite, he is no Philosopher. Those who are on the path of error think that the business of dissolving bodies is a light task; they believe that Gold, once immersed in **Sophic Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio Sophico*; "Philosophical Mercury," the secret, purified solvent of the alchemists</note>, is to be devoured in the blink of an eye—poorly understanding that passage by **Count Bernard of Treviso** regarding his "golden book" irrevocably immersed in the fountain. <note>Bernard of Treviso (1406–1490) was a legendary Italian alchemist. His "Parabola of the Fountain" is a famous allegory describing the dissolution of the "King" (Gold) in a specialized bath.</note>

But how difficult a work it is to dissolve bodies can be attested to by those who have sweated over this **dissolution** <note>original: *dissolutioni*; the process of reducing a solid metal to its primary liquid matter</note>. I myself, who have often been taught these things by the testimony of my own eyes, attest that it requires great skill to rule the fire after the material is prepared, so that it might properly dissolve the bodies without burning the **tinctures** <note>original: *tincturarum*; the potent, soul-like essences of the metals that provide color and transformative power</note>.

Pay attention, therefore, to my teaching: take the body that I have shown you and place it in the "water of our sea" <note>A poetic term for the alchemical solvent</note>, and cook it continually with the proper fire, so that dew and clouds may ascend, and drops may fall back day and night without intermission. 

And you should know that in this **circulation** <note>original: *circulatione*; the repetitive cycle of evaporation and condensation within a sealed glass vessel</note>, the Mercury ascends in its original nature, while the body is left below in its original nature, until, over a long period of time, the body begins to retain some of the water; and thus each participates in the qualities of the other. Because all of the water does not ascend through **sublimation** <note>original: *sublimationem*; the process where a substance is heated into vapor and then collects again as a solid or liquid</note>, but a part of it remains below with the body at the bottom of the vessel, the body is therefore boiled and strained within the subsiding water with vigilant persistence. By this means, the falling drops pierce through the remaining mass, and the water, having been made more subtle by constant circulation, as— <note>The text ends mid-sentence; the next word would be "tanquam" (just as/like).</note>

<summary>This page warns that the alchemical dissolution of gold is a slow, difficult process requiring precise heat, correcting those who believe it happens instantly. It describes the "circulation" process where the solvent repeatedly evaporates and rains back down to gradually soften and "pierce" the solid matter.</summary>

it gently and sweetly extracts the soul of the sun <note>original: "solis animam"; in alchemy, the Sun represents Gold or the active, masculine principle of Sulfur</note>: thus, with the soul acting as a mediator, the spirit is reconciled with the body, and a union of both occurs in the color black. This operation is called the **governance of Mercury**, because Mercury <term>Mercury: representing the fluid, volatile principle or 'philosophical quicksilver'</term> circulates upwards, and in it the body of the Sun boils below. In this work, the body is passive until the appearance of colors, which appear sparingly around the twentieth day amidst a good and continuous boiling. These colors thereafter increase, multiply, and vary until they reach fulfillment in the **blackest blackness** <term>Nigredo: the stage of putrefaction or 'death' of the material, which alchemists believed was essential for rebirth</term>, which the fiftieth day will give to you, if the fates call you.

## CHAPTER XXV.

### *Concerning the second governance of the work, which is*
### *Saturn’s.*

When the governance of Mercury is completed—whose task is to strip the king of his golden garments and to provoke the Lion <note>original: "Leonem"; the Green Lion often represents the raw mineral or the 'acid' used to dissolve gold</note> through various conflicts even to the point of extreme exhaustion—the governance of Saturn appears next. For GOD wills that the work begun be led to its proper end; and the law of this stage is that the exit of one is the entrance of another, the end of one is the beginning of another. No sooner does the governance of Mercury fade than its successor, Saturn, enters for himself,

And this governance is very uniform in respect to heat, because there is only one color, and that the blackest; and yet no smoke, nor winds, nor any symbol of life is seen; at least sometimes it appears dried, and at other times the compound is beheld boiling like liquid pitch. O sad spectacle and image of eternal death, but to the artist, a sweet message! For it is not just any blackness, but a blackness seen shining beyond the most intense blackness. And when you look down and see the matter swelling like dough, rejoice: for you should know that the life-giving spirit is shut within, which at the time appointed by the Almighty will restore life to these corpses. At least beware of the fire, which you are bound to govern here with sound judgment; and I swear to you in good faith, that if by urging the fire in this governance you cause anything to sublimate <term>Sublimate: to turn a solid directly into vapor; in this stage, the material must remain at the bottom to "rot" properly</term>, you will lose the whole work beyond recovery. Therefore be content to be detained with the good Trevisan <note>Trevisan: Bernard of Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemist who described the dark stage of the work as a period of "imprisonment"</note> in prison for forty days and nights, and permit the tender matter to remain at the bottom, which is the nest of conception; being certain and confident that when the period appointed by the Almighty for this operation has passed, the spirit will rise glorious, and will glorify its body; it will ascend, I say, and will circulate gently and without violence, and it will ascend from the center to the heavens, and descend again from the heavens to the center, and receive the power of the things above and the things below <note>original: "vimque arripiet superiorum & inferiorum"; a direct reference to the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, describing the unification of celestial and terrestrial forces</note>.

# CHAPTER XXVI.

## *On the Reign of Jupiter.*

Jupiter <note>In the alchemical sequence, Jupiter represents the transition from the blackness of Saturn to the whiteness of the Moon. This stage is often called the "Peacock’s Tail" because of the many colors that appear.</note> succeeds black Saturn, and is of a different color. For after the required <term>putrefaction</term> <note>The stage where the matter is decomposed and turns black.</note> and the conception has taken place at the bottom of the vessel, by God's command, you will again see shifting colors and a circulating <term>sublimation</term> <note>A process where the substance is heated into vapor and then condensed back into solid form, "rising" within the glass.</note>. This reign is not a lasting one, nor does it endure for more than three weeks. During this time, every imaginable color will appear, for which no certain explanation can be given. In these days, the "showers" <note>Refers to the condensation dripping back down the sides of the glass vessel, often called "alchemical rain."</note> will multiply day by day, and finally, after all these sights, a most beautiful whiteness—appearing like streaks or hairs—shows itself upon the sides of the vessel. Then rejoice, for you have successfully completed the reign of Jupiter. 

In this reign, let the greatest caution be observed, lest the <term>young crows</term> <note>An alchemical metaphor for the black vapors or substances produced during the Saturn stage.</note>, after they have left their nest, should return to it again. Likewise, do not draw off the "water" so immoderately that the settling earth is deprived of it and left dry and useless at the bottom. Thirdly, do not water your "earth" so excessively that you utterly drown it. A proper management of external heat will remedy all of these potential errors.

<summary>This page describes the "Reign of Jupiter," a three-week transitional stage in the Great Work characterized by shifting colors and the eventual appearance of white streaks on the vessel.</summary>
<keywords>Jupiter, Saturn, putrefaction, sublimation, Peacock's Tail, alchemical colors, young crows, moisture balance, heat management</keywords>

# CHAPTER XXVI.

## *On the Rule of Jupiter.*

After black <term>Saturn<gloss>the alchemical name for the stage of blackness or 'nigredo'</gloss></term> follows <term>Jupiter<gloss>the stage of transition from black to white, often characterized by a 'Peacock’s Tail' of many colors</gloss></term>, who is of a different color. For after the required <term>putrefaction<gloss>the chemical decomposition or 'death' of the matter</gloss></term> and <term>conception<gloss>the union of the philosophical sulfur and mercury</gloss></term> has taken place at the bottom of the vessel, by God's command, you will again see changing colors and a circulating <term>sublimation<gloss>the process where a substance is heated into vapor and then condenses back into a solid state</gloss></term>. This rule is not long-lasting, nor does it last beyond three weeks. At this time, all imaginable colors will appear, for which no certain explanation can be given. Rains <note>In alchemy, 'rains' refer to the condensation and dripping of vapors within the sealed glass vessel.</note> will increase day by day during these days, and finally, after all these things, a most beautiful <term>whiteness<gloss>the 'albedo' stage, signifying the purification of the matter</gloss></term> <note>original: "albedo"</note> like streaks or hairs shows itself on the sides of the vessel. Then rejoice, for you have successfully completed the rule of Jupiter. 

Let the greatest caution be taken in this rule, lest the <term>young of the crows<gloss>a common metaphor for the dark, volatile flakes or soot seen in the 'nigredo' stage</gloss></term> <note>original: "corvorum pulli"</note>, after they have left their nest, return to the same. Likewise, do not draw off the water so immoderately that the subsiding earth lacks it and is left dry and useless at the bottom. Thirdly, do not water your earth so excessively that you completely suffocate it. A good management of the external heat will remedy all these errors.

## CHAPTER XXVII.

### *On the Rule of the Moon.*

After the rule of <term>Jupiter<gloss>the transitional stage of many colors</gloss></term> is completed toward the end of the fourth month, the sign of the waxing **Moon** will appear to you. You should know that the entire rule of Jupiter was dedicated to the washing of <term>Laton<gloss>a name used by alchemists to describe the impure, dark matter or "body" that must be purified</gloss></term> <note>original: "Latoni"</note>. The washing <term>spirit<gloss>the volatile or liquid part of the chemical mixture</gloss></term> is extremely white in its nature, but the body to be washed is black—the blackest of blacks. In its transition toward whiteness, all the intermediate colors appeared; when these are finished, the whole becomes white. However, it does not become perfectly glowing on the first day, but rather it will rise up gradually from white to the most brilliant white.

And know that in this rule, the whole matter becomes like liquid <term>quicksilver<gloss>mercury</gloss></term> <note>original: "argenti vivi"</note> to the sight. This is called the "sealing of the mother in the womb of the infant she has birthed." During this rule, there will be various beautiful, momentary colors that quickly vanish, though they are closer to whiteness than to blackness—just as the colors in the rule of Jupiter shared more in blackness than in whiteness.

Know that within three weeks, the rule of the Moon will be completed. But before it is finished, the compound will take on a thousand forms. For as the fluids increase before every kind of <term>coagulation<gloss>the process of the liquid matter turning into a solid</gloss></term>, it will melt and solidify a hundred times a day. Sometimes it will appear like the eyes of fishes, and sometimes like a tree of pure, most polished silver, with

## CHAPTER XXVIII.
### *On the regime of Venus.*
<note>In alchemy, "Venus" refers to copper, but also represents a specific stage of the process characterized by specific colors or temperatures of the furnace.</note>

Above all, this is marvelous: that our <term>stone<note>The "Philosopher's Stone" in its developing form.</note></term>, now perfected in every way and capable of communicating a perfect tincture, spontaneously humbles itself again and contemplates a new volatility without any laying on of hands <note>original: "citra ullam manuum impositionem"; meaning without manual intervention or the addition of new materials.</note>. If, however, you take it from its vessel, and that same stone is shut into another vessel again after its cooling, it will be attempted in vain to be led further. Neither we nor any of the ancient Philosophers <note>The author uses "Philosophers" to mean the alchemical masters of antiquity.</note> are able to provide a demonstrative reason for this, except that it is done by the will of God. At least here, beware for your fire, because this is the law of the perfected stone: that it be fusible. Therefore, if you provide a fire greater than what is right, the matter will be vitrified <note>turned into glass</note>, and having melted, [it will adhere] to the sides of the vessel

When, therefore, you behold the greenness, know that the <term>germinating power<note>original: "virtus germinativa"; the life-force or biological potential for the stone to "grow" and multiply its power.</note></term> is contained within it. Therefore, beware here, lest that greenness be turned into a foul blackness by [excessive heat...]

## CHAPTER XXIX.

### *On the Reign of Mars.*

Once the reign of Venus <note>In alchemical symbolism, Venus represents a stage often associated with the color green and the metal copper.</note> is finished—whose color was primarily greenish and slightly reddish with a dark purple hue, and sometimes leaden—at which time the <term>philosophic tree</term> <note>A term for the crystalline, branch-like growths that appear in the flask during the chemical reaction.</note> flourished with its variegated branches and leaves, the reign of Mars follows. This stage demonstrates a certain yellowness, mostly diluted with a sort of yellowish-brown, and most gloriously displays the fleeting colors of the rainbow and the peacock's tail. This is a drier state of the compound, in which the matter seems to imitate various masks of forms. A hyacinth-blue color, accompanied by a very light orange, will appear frequently during these days. Here the "sealed mother" rises and is purified in the belly of her infant, so that because of the great purity in which the compound is held, all rot and decay may be banished. Truly, dark colors serve as the foundation throughout this entire reign, while the intermediate colors that emerge are most pleasing to behold. Know now that our "virgin earth" <note>The purified matter at the bottom of the vessel, ready for the final transformation.</note> is undergoing its final cultivation, so that the <term>fruit of the Sun</term> <note>The *Fructus Solis* refers to the final goal: the gold or the completed Philosopher's Stone.</note> may be sown and ripened within it. Therefore, maintain a good heat, and you will see for

This is the true fermentation, which you may repeat again if you wish.

# CHAPTER XXXII.

## *The Imbibition of the Stone.*

I know that many authors understand fermentation in this work to be an internal, invisible agent which they call the "ferment"; by its power, the fleeting and thin spirits are spontaneously thickened without any physical manipulation. They call our aforementioned path of fermentation "cibation" <term>feeding</term> with bread and milk; so says Ripley <note>original: "Riplæus". George Ripley (c. 1415–1490) was a famous English alchemist whose works were highly influential in the 17th century.</note>. I, however, am not accustomed to citing others nor swearing by their words; in a matter known as well to me as to them, I have taken my own liberty. <margin>4</margin>There is, therefore, another operation by which the stone is increased more in weight than in power. That is: take your perfected sulfur <note>In alchemical theory, sulfur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle of matter.</note>, whether white or red, and add to three parts of Sulfur a fourth part of water. After a slight appearance of blackness during a decoction <term>continued heating</term> of six or seven days, your newly added water will be thickened just like your Sulfur. Then add a fourth part—not in proportion to the whole compound (which has already congealed one fourth part), but in proportion to the Sulfur as it was before the first imbibition <term>the process of the stone "drinking" or soaking up liquid</term>. Once this has dried, add more in proportion to the three parts of Sulfur originally weighed before the first soaking. In this proportion, you should soak and congeal <note>original: "congeles"; the process of a liquid becoming solid through cooling or chemical change.</note> three times.

<summary>This page discusses the process of "imbibition," a specific alchemical stage where the stone is "fed" liquid to increase its mass. The author contrasts his practical approach with the theories of George Ripley, emphasizing precise proportions for the "sulfur" and "water."</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, sulfur, imbibition, fermentation, George Ripley, cibation, decoction, coagulation</keywords>

## CHAPTER XXXIII.
### *The Multiplication of the Stone.*

For this, no other labor is required except that the perfected stone be taken, and one part of it be joined with <gloss>3 or 4</gloss> parts—three or at most four—of the Mercury <note>The "Mercury of the first work" refers to the specialized alchemical solvent or "first matter" used at the start of the process.</note> of the first work. All the regimes will pass by with the greatest joy, and you will have the whole enriched with a thousandfold power compared to the stone before its multiplication. And if you attempt this again, you will run through all the regimes in three days, and the medicine <note>The "medicine" is another name for the Philosopher's Stone, viewed as a healing agent for "sick" or base metals.</note> will be exalted another thousandfold in its power of tinging. And if you still desire to repeat it, you will lead the work through all regimes and colors within a natural day <note>A 24-hour period.</note>. The same will happen in a single hour if you attempt it once more. You will eventually not even be able to find the power of your stone; it will be so great that it exceeds the capacity of the intellect, if only in this work you [perfe-] <note>The text cuts off here with the catchword "perfe-", likely leading into "perfectaveris" (you have perfected).</note>

## CHAPTER XXXIV.

### *On the Method of Projection.*

Take your perfected Stone (as previously described), whether it be white or red. According to the quality of the medicine <note>The "medicine" refers to the Philosopher's Stone in its finalized, active form, capable of "healing" base metals into gold.</note>, take four parts of either luminary <note>In alchemical texts, "luminaries" refers to the Sun (Gold) or the Moon (Silver).</note>. Melt these in a clean crucible. Then, add your Stone according to the type of melted luminary—white stone for silver or red stone for gold. Once mixed, pour it into a casting cone; it will form a mass that can be crumbled into powder. Take one part of this mixture and ten parts of well-washed Mercury <term>Mercury: also known as quicksilver; used here as a medium to help the Stone penetrate other metals</term>. Heat the Mercury until it begins to crackle <note>original: "strepere incipiat"</note>, then cast in your mixture. It will penetrate the Mercury in the blink of an eye. Melt it by increasing the fire, and the entire mass will become a medicine of the lower order.

Then, take one part of this and project <term>Projection: the final stage of the alchemical "Great Work," where the Stone is cast upon base metals to transform them into gold or silver</term> it onto any melted and purified metal—in such an amount as your Stone is capable of tinting—and you will have gold or silver so pure that nature itself could produce no better. It is better, however, to perform the projection in stages until the tinting power is exhausted; in this way, it is spread further. This is because when a tiny amount is projected onto a large mass, unless the projection occurs within Mercury, a significant loss of the medicine happens because of the dross <note>original: "scorias," referring to the waste or slag produced during the smelting of metals</note> that clings to impure metals. For this reason, the more thoroughly the metals are purified...

<summary>This page describes the "Method of Projection," providing specific ratios for using the Philosopher's Stone to transform base metals into pure gold or silver. It emphasizes the use of mercury as a medium to prevent the precious "medicine" from being lost in the dross of impure metals.</summary>

### CHAPTER XXXV.

#### On the Multiple Uses of This Art.

Whoever has once perfectly mastered this art, by the blessing of GOD, I know not what else he could desire in this whole world, except that he might serve his GOD continually, safe from all wicked fraud and deceit. It would be vain—indeed, the most vain of all things—to long for the empty praise of the common crowd <note>original: "pompa vulgarem auram anhelare"; literally "to pant for the common breeze of pomp."</note>. Such things do not concern the hearts of those who are skilled in this art; rather, they scorn and despise them. Therefore, for the person who has been blessed by GOD with this talent <term>Talent: used here in the biblical sense of a divinely entrusted treasure or gift</term>, such a field of delight lies open:

First, if he were to live for a thousand years and feed a million <note>original: "millium mille," or a thousand thousands</note> men every day, he would never be in want, for he is able to multiply the Stone according to his wish, both in weight and in power. Thus, if he so desired, he could tint <term>Tinting: the alchemical process of projecting the Stone's "color" and quality onto base metals to transform them into noble ones</term> all the imperfect metals in the world into true gold or silver.

Second, by this art, he will be able to produce precious stones and gems, the likes of which cannot be matched by anything found in nature without the aid of this art.

Third and finally, he possesses the universal medicine for all diseases, so that at least one... <note>The Latin text breaks off mid-word at "sal-"; when combined with the catchword "tem," it forms "saltem," meaning "at least."</note>
