# Preparation of <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: "☿ÿ," the symbol for Mercury</note>

32 The <term>Mastery</term> <note>original: "Magisteriū," referring to the "Great Work" or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> which has been so greatly hidden, is to prepare that which is found everywhere on earth, ready at our hands.

37 The preparation of the true Mercury of the philosophers is so difficult that there is a need for a special grace of God, if anyone should desire to reach an exact knowledge of it. I have presented such details concerning its manufacture that I could not say more, except to give credit to Him for what I have done, provided that the things are called by their own names.

44 In our work, we must find a <term>humidity</term> <note>original: "humiditatē." In alchemy, "humidity" usually refers to a specialized liquid solvent or "water" that does not wet the hands.</note> in which <term>gold</term> <note>original: "aurū"</note> melts like ice in lukewarm water. Toward this end, some have attempted to profit through the price of <term>Gold</term> <note>original: "☉," the symbol for Sol or Gold</note>; but in vain. For in this work, everyone is a liar who sells for a "price of flesh" <note>original: "carō vẽdī precio," likely a warning against those who sell alchemical secrets for worldly profit or through deceptive, physical means.</note>. <margin>B</margin> But they rejected all other things, except for one salt which is the <term>saliva</term> <note>original: "saliva"; in alchemy, this refers to a specialized liquid solvent or "the water that does not wet the hands."</note> of all youth <note>original: "ōms juv̄"; likely "omnis juventutis" (of all youth) or possibly a reference to Jupiter (Tin).</note>, which dissolves any metal, and by the same operation in which it <term>coagulates</term> <note>The process of thickening or solidifying a liquid into a solid state.</note>, and this is done only by a violent path. <margin>2a</margin> but in vain, since the aforementioned work demands <term>mortification</term> <note>The symbolic death or chemical breakdown of a substance into its base components.</note> and <term>regeneration</term> <note>The renewal or "rebirth" of a substance into a higher, more purified form.</note> in equal measure, 
<margin>2b</margin> for which there is work to be done before the main operation. Likewise, it is pure in that which ought to have been active; so that there is a need to introduce to this another life of the same nature, through the introduction of which the hidden life of the Mercury is awakened, and thus life receives life. Then at last, fundamentally changed, the dross is cast out from the center. This life exists in metallic Mercury alone: which is sought in vain in any prepared Mercury or in <term>common gold</term> <note>original: "aurū vulgare"; standard gold used for currency, which alchemists believed was "dead" compared to the "living" gold of the philosophers.</note>, even though it might have the power to separate dross from mature gold; for to purge those things is...

# Preparation II

Ready to blend together, and the outcome responded to their wishes; they mixed the liquids <gloss>all</gloss> of life, <gloss>this</gloss> of life; dry things are moistened <gloss>this</gloss> through the active, and the passive things are sharpened; and through the living, the dead things are brought to life.

<margin>A</margin> Thus the sky was clouded over <note>original: "obnubilatu"; a metaphor for the *Nigredo* or blackening stage of the work where the matter decomposes.</note> for a time, which <margin>B</margin> after heavy rains became clear again <note>original: "serenu"; refers to the *Albedo* or whitening stage, where the matter is purified.</note>. Here the hermaphrodite <note>original: "hermaphroditicus"; a symbolic figure representing the union of the "male" (Sulfur) and "female" (Mercury) principles.</note> emerged. This also they placed in the fire, and they coagulated it for a very long time, and in its coagulation they found the purest sun <note>Gold</note> and moon <note>Silver</note>.

Finally, returning to their thoughts, they considered that what was here purified <unclear>and</unclear> coagulated was a metal, but having been made volatile, it left no residue remaining in the bottom after being distilled again; for this reason they named it their immature sun and their living moon <note>The "Philosophical Mercury," a substance that contains the potential for gold but is still in a liquid or volatile state.</note>. They considered here that since it was the true essence of gold, yet still existing in a volatile state, why should it not be the field <note>original: "ager"; the matrix or "soil" in which the alchemical seed is planted.</note> in which the healthy sun might be increased in virtue? For that reason they placed the sun within the same, and that which was fixed was made volatile in it, the hard became soft, the coagulated was dissolved, while nature herself stood amazed. Wherefore they betrothed these two to one another, and enclosed them in glass.

### 2nd Preparation

[When one] has placed the work at the **Fire** <note>represented by the symbol $\triangle$</note> and guided the operation according to

<margin>B</margin> If indeed with the **Sun** it gently liquefies the waves here, and dissolves them with a heat tempered to the requirements of the composite,
<margin>27</margin> by that same fire they coagulate themselves and give their own coagulation to the **Sun**. It is incredible, yet true, that **Mercury**, being homogeneous, pure, and clean, [is made] pregnant with internal **Fire** through our artifice,

# 5. Second Preparation

By heat alone, it even coagulates itself in the manner of the **flower of milk** <note>original: "floris lactis"; this refers to the way cream rises and thickens on the surface of milk</note>, floating above as a subtle earth upon the waters. But when made with the **sun** <note>original: "sole"; in alchemical texts, "the sun" almost always refers to gold</note>, the body is seen to be not only not coagulated, but daily softer, until the spirit of the almost dissolved body has begun to coagulate into a **very black color** <note>the *nigredo* or "blackness" phase, a stage of putrefaction and transition in alchemy</note> and a **most foul odor**. It is evident from this that through this metallic spirit is truly the **moving force** <note>original: "movens for̄"; the active principle or form that drives the transformation</note> through which the whole thing turns and revolves in a circle. And this is truly the <term>volatile gold</term> <note>Gold that has been purified or processed until it can be vaporized or dissolved into a "spiritual" state</note> while it is sufficiently **digested** <note>the process of slow, steady heating</note> and sufficiently pure; therefore, by a clean digestion, it passes into the sun. But if they flee to the sun in the perfect ones, it is not so much coagulated, but it dissolves the **corporeal gold**, and with it dissolved, it remains under **one form**.

33. The Author affirms that the **gold of the common people** <note>original: "aurū vulgi"; standard metallic gold as opposed to the "philosophical" gold of the chemists</note> is the <unclear>material</unclear> of the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold</note>...
34. I say that only **gold** [is used] and that it is of a **material nature**.
37. We show gold in the **sun** alone without metaphor.

Therefore, <term>quicksilver</term> <note>mercury</note>, declared without any other thing, first perfected by nature and available for sale, we can now manufacture through **art** <note>the "Art" is a standard term for the practice of alchemy</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on the "Second Preparation." It focuses on the distinction between "common gold" (the metal used by the public) and the "Gold of the Wise" (the philosophical substance required for the Great Work). The author discusses the role of the "metallic spirit" and the necessity of "digestion" to create the "tincture" of gold.</meta>

44.

A. This work is made from our sole **spirit** <note>original: "$\mathcal{g}^o$"; likely an abbreviation for *spiritus* or *genus*, referring to the essential active principle</note>... a golden spirit from our spirit and from **common gold** <note>original: "auro vulgi"; standard metallic gold</note> produced through a **rotated general motion** <note>original: "per $g\overline{im}$ $m\overline{u}$ rotato"; likely referring to the circular circulation of matter in a vessel</note>.

B. This gold is the **metallic spirit** <note>original: "$\mathcal{g}^y$ metalliū"</note>, having been **drunk by fire** <note>original: "igne potatū"; purified or refined through intense heat</note>, which is called "Fire 16." <unclear>Near</unclear> the gold which is spoken of in "Power 17" and which is truly "Gold 17." This gold is said to be from common gold, which is called here a **general substance** <note>original: "$g\overline{im}$"; likely *generali*</note>; without it, a profitable **coagulation** <note>the process of a liquid or vapor becoming solid</note> would never be received, and no <term>tincture</term> <note>an alchemical essence capable of transmuting or "coloring" base metals into gold</note> could be made. Although both parts of the work are made more easily without common gold, the author nevertheless expressly states here that in the **Gold of the Wise** <note>original: "auro sophorū"; the "philosophical gold" used by alchemists rather than commercial gold</note> lies the **tincture of goldness**.

<margin>30</margin>

C. Because that metallic spirit, although it may be sufficiently **digested** <note>original: "digestū"; the process of slow, steady heating in a sealed container, similar to incubation</note>, is nonetheless called "most digested" by reason of the spirit which it has digested. --- Page 225 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of "living gold" mentioned in the previous index. It shifts from a list of references to a practical and philosophical explanation of how "dead" metal is brought to life through its union with "philosophical mercury."</meta>

->Preface: A difficult explanation<- <note>original: "Praes: difficil: evlog"; likely an abbreviation for "Praescriptio difficilis eulogium" or a similar introductory heading.</note>

<margin>32</margin> The property of one [substance] joined with the life of the other becomes one and the same in their cycle. From this, it is clear that gold is taken as if it were dead, while the water <note>In alchemy, "water" usually refers to the liquid <term>mercury</term> or a specialized solvent, not common water.</note> is taken as living. Therefore, because of the body, these two—through a brief <term>decoction</term> <note>A process of heating or boiling a substance in a liquid to extract its essence.</note>—together cause the dead gold to come to life, and the living mercury is killed. <note>This "killing" and "reviving" refers to a chemical transformation where the liquid mercury loses its fluidity (dies) to stabilize the gold.</note>
In this mercury, we cook this gold—whether it be in the form of filings or thin plates—precisely into an <term>amalgam</term>. The gold is dissolved by our conquering water and returns to its **prime matter**, in which the enclosed life of the gold is set free; it then receives the life of the dissolving mercury. At that point, the mercury is to the gold exactly what the earth is to the wheat. <note>A classic alchemical metaphor: mercury acts as the fertile soil, and gold acts as the seed that must "die" in the earth to sprout into something new.</note> This, namely, is the logic of our work and our entire **philosophy**.

<margin>33</margin> Therefore, nothing in our work is secret except for the mercury alone. The mastery of this involves preparing it with "life," marrying it to the gold in the correct proportion, and governing the fire according to the requirements of the mercury.
But he who has rightly prepared the mercury with its spirit and has joined the gold with it in such a way [without understanding this life], his gold remains merely the gold of the common people. This is because it joins with such a partner with difficulty, and within it, all things remain unchanged—as if they were merely sitting in a chest. <note>The author warns that without the "vivification" of the mercury, the metals just sit together like objects in a box (the "ark") rather than transforming.</note>

<summary>This page explains the "vivification" of gold, describing how "dead" gold is revived by "living" mercury to return to its prime matter. It emphasizes that the true secret of alchemy is the preparation of the mercury, which serves as the fertile soil for the gold seed.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, mercury, gold, vivification, prime matter, decoction, philosophical marriage, transmutation</keywords>

# A Difficult and Longer Process

<margin>50</margin> If you have worked with <term>our mercury</term> <note>original: "mercurio nostro"; a specialized solvent or "secret" mercury claimed by alchemists to be the true base of the Work, distinct from the liquid metal found in nature.</note> and <term>common gold</term> <note>original: "auro vulgi"; ordinary gold used in jewelry or coinage, which alchemists believed must be "opened" to release its inner spirit.</note> according to the <term>proper regimen</term> <note>original: "regimine debito"; the precise management of heat and time during the alchemical process.</note>, after those 150 days you will possess our gold; which is not a new thing made from our mercury; for if common gold has been broken down into its elements by our mercury and then joined together again, the entire mixture, through the benefit of fire, will become our gold; and if they boil it again with mercury, it will display all the signs described by the <term>philosophers</term> <note>original: "philosophis"; the historical masters of alchemy, such as Geber or Hermes Trismegistus.</note> using such a fire as they themselves <margin>B</margin> described.

Now, truly, if you apply the <term>regimen of the stone</term> <note>original: "regimen lapidis"; the specific sequence of heating required to produce the Philosopher's Stone.</note> to the boiling of <term>common sun</term> <note>original: "solis vulgi"; a synonym for common gold, often symbolized by the sun (☉).</note>, however pure it may be, with our mercury, <margin>C</margin> you are on the path of error. And that is the great labyrinth in which almost all <margin>51</margin> <term>beginners</term> <note>original: "tyrones"; novices or apprentices who have not yet mastered the hidden meanings of alchemical texts.</note> get stuck: because the philosophers write about both ways, which are in fact only one way, except that one is more direct than the other. Therefore, those of us who write about common gold in this little treatise—just as <term>Artephius</term>, <term>Flamel</term>, <term>Ripley</term>, etc., have done—are not to be understood in any other way than this: that the <term>philosophical sun</term> <note>original: "sol philosophicus"; the perfected, spiritualized gold of the alchemists.</note> is made from common gold and our mercury, which then, through repeated melting, will yield <term>Jupiter</term> <note>The symbol ♃; in this context, it likely refers to a specific gray or metallic stage of the transformation, or the metal tin as a symbolic stepping stone.</note> and a <term>fixed quicksilver</term> <note>original: "argentum vivum fixum"; mercury that has been "fixed" so that it no longer evaporates when exposed to heat</note> that is incombustible and firm.

<summary>This page describes the "Difficult Process" of breaking down common gold using a specialized "philosophical mercury" to create alchemical gold, warning that many beginners get lost in the metaphorical "labyrinth" of the two paths.</summary>

Similarly, by this way of attainment, **our stone** <note>original: "lapis noster"; the Philosopher’s Stone, the ultimate goal of the alchemist.</note> is present in every metal and mineral. # Process of the Difficult and Long [Path]? R

Likewise, and through this method of binding, **our stone** <term>lapis noster: the Philosopher's Stone in its developing or potential state.</term> is in every metal and mineral. Insofar as pure **common gold** <note>original symbol: ☉; "common gold" refers to the physical metal, as opposed to the "philosophical gold" the alchemist seeks to create.</note> can be extracted from them, from which **our gold** can be more closely sought. # Practice of the Easier Secret
## Antimony and Gold II

A Let a <term>regulus</term> <note>original: "regulus"; the metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible during smelting. In this context, it likely refers to the "Star Regulus of Antimony," a crystalline form of the metal prized by alchemists.</note> be made.
B Let it be purified.
C Then let the gold be purified.
D Let the said Regulus and gold be joined.
E Let these two <term>digest</term> <note>original: "digerant"; the process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat, similar to digestion in a stomach, to mature the matter.</note>.
F Let them distill or <term>sublime</term> <note>original: "subliment"; the process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used to purify the "spirit" of the metal.</note> twenty or four times here <unclear>preceding</unclear> above the regulus in the conversions and digestions.

45 There is another operation by which the <term>stone</term><note>original: "lapis"; the Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical work.</note> is increased in weight more than in power. Cook 3 parts of your perfected <term>gold</term><gloss>gold</gloss><note>original: symbol $\odot$, representing the Sun or gold, here likely meaning the "Philosophical Gold" or the perfected red elixir.</note> in 4 parts of <term>water</term><note>original: "aquae"; in this context, "water" refers to the "mercurial water" or the philosophical solvent used to dissolve the stone.</note>, and after a little <term>blackness</term><note>original: "nigredinis"; the *Nigredo* stage, signifying the dissolution and "death" of the matter before its rebirth.</note> of 6 or 7 days, the water will thicken like your <unclear>mercury</unclear><note>original: symbol $\curlywedge$; this symbol often represents a specific solvent or "menstruum" used in the dissolution process.</note>. Then add as much water as you added previously from those 4 parts; and thus <term>imbibe</term><note>original: "imbibe"; the process of "feeding" the dry stone with liquid until it is fully saturated.</note> three times and <term>congeal</term><note>original: "congela"; the process of solidifying the liquid matter through gentle heat.</note>. Finally, with the 5th part of water placed upon your <term>gold</term><gloss>gold</gloss>, on the seventh <term>imbibition</term> place it over the former fire; govern the fire so that the work is completed at most within one month. Then you have the true stone of the <term>second order</term><note>original: "3ij ordinis"; alchemists classified the stone by "orders" based on its strength; a second-order stone is more potent than the first, capable of higher ratios of transmutation.</note>, of which one part <term>tints</term><note>original: "tingit"; refers to the process of "dyeing" or transmuting a base metal into gold or silver.</note> ten thousand.

48 But truly, with the stone [treated] in this way, it is multiplied in a <unclear>far</unclear> shorter time and by our more gentle fire.

# The Author’s Warnings

I warn that what I have said must be understood with a grain of salt <note>original: "cum grano salis"</note>, lest by chance the learned happen to err too often as they proceed. For we have woven these <term>physical subtleties</term><note>original: "physicas subtilitates"; here referring to the hidden laws of nature and alchemy rather than modern physics.</note> with such unusual clarity that unless you have encountered the many metaphors in the preceding chapters, you will gather hardly any harvest.

For example, where we spoke without any ambiguity of one thing through priority, which is in every other; one which is available for sale, and the other manufactured by art:

know that our substance gives gold of its own accord (because it is changed [either] by the mixture); for if you do not know what that gold is—the secret of our subtleties—(because <unclear>it...</unclear> after its cooking is finished, it is not yet the gold of the <term>philosophers</term><note>original: "philosophes"; the "Adepts" or master alchemists who possess the true secret of the Stone.</note> which performs <term>projection</term><note>original: "projection"; the final stage of the Great Work where the completed Elixir is cast upon base metals to transmute them into gold.</note>. But it is only the immediate manner [see pages] 49, 50, 51.), it is necessary that you sell it for the sun of the common people <note>original: "sole vulgi"; a common alchemical term for ordinary, commercial gold as opposed to the living "Philosophical Gold."</note>; for it is in

# The Author’s Warnings

If, upon examination, the Gold <note>original: ☉, the symbol for the Sun and Gold.</note> is true; and thus to know the Gold commonly for sale, but Gold commonly bought—in order that it may become ours, there is a need for our art. Therefore, our Gold is the first matter of our Stone; common Gold is its proximate <note>The "proximate" matter is a substance that is already close to the desired final state but still requires transformation.</note> matter; other metals are remote matters; and those things which are not metallic are most remote, or rather, alien. # The Author’s Warnings

Fire is released, without which the work cannot be maturely perfected. <gloss>Wisdom</gloss> <note>original: "Sophia"</note>

<margin>52</margin> Know that this alone is the difficulty: that everyone varies the procedure <note>original: "regimen"; in alchemy, this refers to the precise management of heat and time during the transformation of matter.</note> for a single goal, and when they speak of one work, they teach the procedure of another.

<margin>56</margin> I swear to you in good faith that they hide nothing except the procedure; however, if you are prudent, you will easily gather this from my words.

<margin>61</margin> Nothing in the entire work of the Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note> is to be hidden except the procedure, and if they were to propose it openly, fools would laugh at the art. For once that is known, the whole thing is nothing else than "women’s work and children’s play" <note>original: "opus mulierum et ludus puerorum"; a common alchemical phrase suggesting that once the initial secret is discovered, the remaining work is as simple—though perhaps as tedious—as domestic chores.</note>; yet to boil it down <margin>67</margin> wherefore

<margin>62</margin> know that there is one procedure in the whole work, a single line; yet to boil down and to digest, and in this one procedure, many other things are embraced, which the jealous <note>original: "invidi"; referring to the "Envious Philosophers" who supposedly obscured their writings to prevent the unworthy from attaining the secret.</note> have described as if they were various operations.

---

See other warnings on pages 50, 51, 53.

<summary>This page describes the paradoxical nature of "Our Water," characterizing it as a union of many substances that must be purged of "leprous" impurities. It uses complex allegories involving the "thief," the "hermaphrodite," and "hydrophobia" (fear of water) to explain the chemical transition from a dry, solid state to a purified, dissolved one.</summary>

In February 1690, Jacob Collins, a <insert>Dutch</insert> Englishman, told me that the son of Van Helmont <note>Francis Mercury van Helmont (1614–1698), a Flemish alchemist and physician known for his work on the "alphabet of nature."</note> had made this book. He worked by the "dry path" <note>The "dry path" (voye seiche) in alchemy typically refers to a process involving high heat and crucibles rather than liquid solvents in glass vessels.</note> in <note>original symbol: +; likely referring to crucibles</note> without using glass.

In the journal of 1688, folio 338, there is a book titled *The Revealer of the Great Philosophical Secret, in which Hermetic disciples and great seekers of the Work will find the way to their labor most clearly explained* <note>original Latin: *Magni Philosophici Arcani Revelator quo hermetici discipuli magnique scrutatores operis via ad suum laborem mora clarissimè explicata inveniet*</note> — in duodecimo <note>A book size where the sheet is folded into 12 leaves.</note>, Geneva and Paris, at the house of Daniel Hortemels, 1688.

In April or May 1696, Monsieur l'Abbe du Mottier told me that this person had given him the books of Robert Fludd <note>An English Paracelsian physician and mystic.</note>, the Cosmopolite <note>A pseudonym for the famous alchemist Michael Sendivogius.</note>, and the Testament of Lully <note>Ramon Llull, a Majorcan philosopher to whom many alchemical works were pseudographically attributed.</note>, and especially the ocular experiment <note>original: *experiment oculaire*</note> of Fludd, which is his last work.

The aforementioned Francis <unclear>Alberr</unclear>, a <unclear>miller</unclear> in England in a farm at <unclear>P...mill</unclear>, found a lead box where there were two bottles with the Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold.</note> and a manuscript containing the process that he practiced; he traveled and left his wife at Bagshot, 1700 <unclear>over on the...</unclear>

On the signs of the work, chapters 27, ~~29~~ 30 <note>original Latin: *de signis operis*. In alchemy, "signs" are the specific physical changes—often color shifts like the black "raven's head" or the white "swan"—that indicate the progress of the transmutation.</note>
In the single metallic kingdom of the origin of the mineral, [page] 51. <note>original Latin: *in uno regno metallico originis mineræ*. This reflects the alchemical belief in a "monarchy" of metals, where all species originate from a single base substance or "seed."</note>
Appearances in the vessels, [page] 59 # <note>original Latin: *appariciones i vasis*. This refers to the visual phenomena, such as bubbling, crystallization, or vapors, seen within the sealed glass flasks during the "Work."</note>
Fermentation and also multiplication, [page] 74 <unclear/> 75, and chapter 33. <note>original Latin: *Fermentacio ac etiam multiplicatio*. Fermentation is the penultimate stage where the "Stone" is fixed with a specific nature; multiplication is the final process of increasing its potency and quantity.</note>
